# Norfolk Submarine Pier 3 Utility Services - ground truth

complete_with_documented_source_limitations

Source: [pdf/02__vol2__015__Norfolk_Submarine_Pier_3_Utility_Services.pdf](../../pdf/02__vol2__015__Norfolk_Submarine_Pier_3_Utility_Services.pdf). 27 supplied pages. SHA-256 `7ac5b4d6d97b5416f7d04a2a3abd303513ac9f19cec5930d0b9aa3bcd550da97`.

Complete supplied 27-page mechanical delivery, not the absent 573 sheets of the 600-sheet project. All unique scheduled and additional printed equipment identities, all schedule cells/notes, every printed BAS row and explicit typical application, every sheet/view scale, and the complete primary-plan space-control-symbol inventory are retained. Generic untagged commodity quantities, exact field terminal assignments, and values not established by the PDF remain explicitly unknown rather than invented.

## Reading this record

- Schedule rows, equipment identities, outlet types, installed devices and logical BAS applications are different quantities.
- 24 equipment schedules contain 105 rows and 109 building-scoped tag identities, including 10 repeated outlet types. The other 99 scheduled identities each have a primary plan anchor. An additional 19 printed identities include plan-only equipment, controls-referenced equipment and the EG-3 outlet type.
- 15 actuated-water-valve rows and 21 motorized-damper rows are included. No standalone actuator schedule is present. This is not a claim that only 36 actuator motors exist; torque/model and hardware multiplicity are not supplied.
- 117 printed/template BAS rows normalize to 31 AI / 43 DI / 10 AO / 33 DO. 26 explicit applications contain 192 logical occurrences: 48 AI / 72 DI / 13 AO / 59 DO. These are not physical field terminals; shared/unmarked sensors and grouped dampers prevent a defensible exact installed I/O count.
- 23 drawn space-control marks: 21 T and 2 S. One further gatehouse DSS-3 DDC sensor is required but not separately located. CO2 sensors are absent from the supplied PDF; internet-connected smart-thermostat capability is not stated.
- Table rows retain literal units, comparison operators, empty cells, alarm/trend flags and source errors. A source conflict is not silently corrected by a plausible engineering assumption.
- All source bounds are displayed-page PDF points, top-left origin. Text-region bounds locate evidence, not equipment outlines. Parser agreement corroborates text and location; visual review establishes drawn relationships.
- The component files are corroborated parts of this document. This assembled record's requirement audit and current verification determine document status; no individual component substitutes for the complete audit.

## Per-application point ledger

192 is the sum of explicitly scoped typical applications, including shared-reference occurrences. It is NOT unique hardware, wired I/O, installed controller terminals or a procurement total. Four MPAC-relief AI-1 occurrences refer to the already counted FC-3 temperature source. FC-1/EF-1 require extra temperature inputs not separately numbered. Grouped dampers and unlisted safety/humidity functions prevent an honest exact physical terminal total.

## Schedules

All populated equipment/reference schedule rows on AM601, AM602 and AM603. Rows are not physical installed quantities; repeated tag names must retain building scope. Plan-only tags, control matrices, sheet metadata and zone counts remain separate unfinished audit sections.

### USB-FC - PDF 18

4 printed rows. Exact original cells below; units follow authored column keys and header witnesses.

- audit notes:
  - 15 valve rows. No actuator torque/model/electrical-power columns. VLV-15 location USB4 conflicts with FC-4's USB5 schedule location.
  - Diagrams use V-1 through V-15, not VLV- prefix. AM702 places AO-1 on bypass V-3, not V-1. VLV-3 is scheduled TWO POSITION while AM702/AM703 require modulating bypass control: preserve this source conflict. AM610 shows NO on V-1, V-2 and V-4 through V-10, while AM702 shows NC on those isolation valves; fail/normal position requires clarification.

- sequences:
  - Record:
    - id: cw
    - page: 24
    - sheet: AM703
    - view: B4 CONDENSER WATER CONTROLS
    - bbox:
      - 1495
      - 85
      - 2220
      - 1040
    - diagram:
      - page: 23
      - sheet: AM702
      - view: A1 CONDENSER WATER DIAGRAM
    - equipment:
      - CCC-1
      - CCC-2
      - P-1
      - P-2
      - MPAC-1
      - MPAC-2
      - MPAC-3
      - MPAC-4
      - HPAC-1
      - HPAC-2
      - HPAC-3
      - V-1 through V-11
    - logic:
      - PI control unless otherwise specified. Below low limit, coolers/control loops disabled and bypass open for building circulation; above low limit, enable lead cooler and modulate bypass using pre-pump CWR temperature AI-6.
      - At 100% bypass opening reset high limit to 90 F. Spray pump engages at reset setpoint minus 4 F; cooler fans engage above reset plus 2 F and modulate. Reverse on falling CWR temperature.
      - With no compressors enabled: pumps/coolers off, minimum bypass open and lead/lag shutoff valves closed. With compressors enabled: start lead condenser-water pump, open associated compressor isolation valves and modulate pump flow to the operating-combination table.
      - After compressors stop, ramp pump to minimum speed, modulate minimum-flow bypass and retain pump operation for 15 minutes adjustable.
      - On lead cooler or pump failure de-energize failed equipment and energize standby pump as written; alternate lead/lag on weekly schedule and runtime.
    - setpoints:
      - Record:
        - name: CWR low limit
        - value: 55
        - unit: F
        - adjustable: true
      - Record:
        - name: CWR high limit
        - value: 80
        - unit: F
        - adjustable: true
      - Record:
        - name: CWR high-limit reset
        - value: 90
        - unit: F
        - adjustable: true
    - alarms:
      - CWR setpoint +3 F for 15 min
      - CWR setpoint -3 F for 15 min
      - Lead cooler failure
      - Lead pump failure
      - Any compressor CWR temperature 110 degrees or higher
    - source conflicts:
      - Mode paragraph mixes CWS, CWR and ZN variable names. Low-limit paragraph references AI-1, while setpoint list uses AI-6 and calls it MAIN CWR TEMPERATURE; the points matrix calls AI-1 MAIN CWR and AI-6 CWR BEFORE PUMPS.
      - V-3 is modulated by AO-1 in AM702/AM703; VLV-3 in AM602 is scheduled TWO POSITION.
      - AM610 shows NO on V-1, V-2 and V-4 through V-10; AM702 shows NC at those valves. V-3 and V-11 show NO on both.
      - Immediate no-compressor shutdown paragraph must be reconciled with the subsequent 15-minute pump overrun, rather than dropping either requirement.
      - Failure text specifies standby PUMP even when describing cooler failure. Do not silently substitute a complete standby-cooler sequence.
    - assertion ids:
      - cw-pi
      - cw-mode
      - cw-modulation
      - cw-overrun
      - cw-failure
      - cw-alarms
      - cw-setpoints
    - operating flow table: CW-OPERATING-FLOW
  - Record:
    - id: fc
    - page: 25
    - sheet: AM704

    - logic:
      - Heat enable below low-limit temperature with 2 F deadband; otherwise fan/control loops disabled. Source also says to disable cooling-coil loops although scheduled units have heating coils/electric backup.
      - Below 80 F condenser-water temperature, close hot-water valve and enable electric heat to maintain 55 F.
      - FC-1 requires two temperature sensors, selecting the lowest reading for heating and backup.
      - Modulate heating valve to maintain zone temperature when enabled; close when disabled. Direct-wired fan-starter safeties, monitor proof, disable and stop fan until reset on failure.
    - setpoints:

      - Above 80 F zone temperature, open outside-air damper, prove it open, then start exhaust fan. Falling temperature with 5 F deadband stops fan and closes damper.
      - EF-1 requires two temperature sensors; highest reading governs ventilation.

    - logic:
      - At zone temperature above 80 F open one of three intake dampers and run EF-6 at minimum speed; modulate fan speed to maintain temperature. At falling temperature with 5 F deadband stop EF-6 and close active damper.
      - Rotate open intake weekly. Next damper must open before current one closes. If a damper fails, open the next in rotation; send critical alarm on open/close failure.
      - ATS-2 closed OR either/both generators running: open all three intake dampers and de-energize EF-6.
      - Direct-hardwired safeties and proof monitoring stop controls until reset on failure.

      - Proof/safety failure disables control loops; monitor airflow and both dampers.

      - Damper failure

      - Auxiliary drain pan float switch shuts unit down on water detection (shown in diagram, not listed as a separate marked BAS point).

- interpretation rules:
  - AM001 labels circle T as THERMOSTAT and circle S as TEMPERATURE SENSOR. Keep printed symbol type separate from DDC functional role; do not rename every T to an internet-connected thermostat.
  - For FC/EF/UH T marks, the controls diagrams and AI-1 rows describe a zone-temperature input. The source does not supply a controller/terminal address per physical sensor.
  - FC-1 and EF-1 each span the fire-pump and fresh-water-pump spaces and explicitly require two temperature sensors; those are four distinct drawn marks, not duplicate labels.
  - MPAC relief AI-1 reuses FC-3 temperature and adds no new space sensor.
  - Typical control drawings show representative sensor symbols; they are not extra plan instances.
  - Input points do not prove separate hardware where shared or communicated. Preserve required functions, drawn devices and field-wired counts separately.

## Reference Tables

Non-equipment reference tables outside AM601-AM603. These rows are criteria/detail types, never installed quantities.

### INDOOR-DESIGN-CRITERIA - PDF 1

| 2 | GATEHOUSE | 78°F | 50% RH | 57.9°F | 68°F | NC* |
| 3 | ROOM USB8 TELECOM ROOM & ROOM USB9 FIRE ALARM CLOSET | 78°F | 50% RH | 57.9°F | 68°F | NC* |
| 4 | ALL OTHER UNOCCUPIED SPACES | UC | UC | UC | 55°F | NC* |

## Equipment Reconciliation

Authored equipment identity, primary-plan anchors, schedule/diagram aliases, outlet multiplicities and source conflicts. Schedule specifications remain in their complete cell grids; this module references those rows rather than creating a second contradictory copy. Text bounds corroborate labels; visual tracing establishes relationships and is not falsely described as parser inference.

Each referenced schedule row retains all columns, notes and units in schedules.json or supplemental_schedules.json. Indoor/outdoor split pairs contain two equipment identities in one row. No quantity is derived from the largest suffix.

Condenser-water and EF-6 owner_groups in points.json retain source ownership, including the MPAC status conflict; valve aliases and generator_damper_mapping refine service without adding points.

CCC assembly groups include their isolation-valve command, also cross-referenced in equipment_reconciliation.valve_aliases; that alias is a reference to the SAME point, not an additional DO. BI-18/19/20 matrix descriptions literally say MPAC-1; diagram labels BI-18/19/20 at MPAC-2/3/4. Both are retained with no silent correction.

## Verification

MuPDF 1.27.2.2 and Poppler 26.03.0 independently captured all 27 pages. Current modules contain 1752 cell assertions and 710 bounded text assertions; each is checked in both engines, with additional header witnesses and consistency/absence checks. Independent text agreement is not automatic semantic interpretation or symbol counting. Firm/seal outline lettering is visually corroborated using separate engine renderings. The coordinator separately reviewed all source pages and relationships. Generic untagged commodity quantities and missing/conflicting source facts are not fabricated.

Re-run: `python3 ground_truth/tools/bundle_record.py verify ground_truth/records/02__vol2__015.json` from the collection directory.
